It performs no\ndatabase access. The load command (FEATURE-LoadStore.md) maps `RejectedFile` to exit 3.\n\n## Rules\n\n1. The header's first field is `Date`; every other field is a three-letter uppercase currency code.\n   The ECB writes a trailing comma on each line; the resulting empty last field is ignored.\n2. Each row has exactly as many fields as the header, and begins with a date `YYYY-MM-DD` that is a\n   real calendar date and is not repeated within the file.\n3. A value is `N/A` (no rate, nothing returned) or matches `[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?` and is nonzero.\n4. The whole file is checked before the function returns; any violation raises `RejectedFile`, a\n   subclass of `Exception`.\n5. Rates are returned as the published text, never as a number.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC parse-valid-file |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261027 V1 |\n| Description | `fxrates load <csv>` validates an ECB history file, stores its rates as text in one transaction, and reports the dates and rates counts. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-LoadValidation.md, FEATURE-InitCommand.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides | fxrates load |\n| Consumes | fxrates_app.ecb.parse_history, rates.replace_rate, fxrates_app.config.database_path, cli.main |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`fxrates load <csv>` reads an ECB history file, validates it completely with\n`fxrates_app.ecb.parse_history`, and then upserts every non-N/A rate through `rates.replace_rate`\nin a single transaction, so a reader sees all or none of the load.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- A rejected file (`RejectedFile`) exits `3`, prints nothing to standard output, writes one\n  diagnostic line to standard error, and stores nothing.\n- On success it prints `dates,rates` on the first line and `<dates>,<rates>` on the second, where\n  `dates` counts the dates in the file with at least one stored rate and `rates` counts the rates stored.\n- A rate for a (date, currency) already stored replaces it, so loading the same file twice leaves the\n  database unchanged.\n- A file holding only N/A values prints `0,0` and stores nothing.\n- Unreadable files are handled by FEATURE-LoadUnreadable.md.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-RATE-001` — write: store a rate, replacing the stored one - supplies: `date`, `currency`, `rate` - returns: `date`, `currency`, `rate`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC load-counts-and-text ===\nIntent: A valid file exits 0, reports the dates and rates counts derived from the fixture (an all-N/A date is not counted), and stores each rate as published text.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nimport csv\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nheader = [\"Date\", \"ZZA\", \"ZZB\", \"ZZH\"]\nrows = [\n    [\"1900-08-03\", \"1.50\", \"N/A\", \"N/A\"],\n    [\"1900-08-02\", \"0.943\", \"2\", \"N/A\"],\n    [\"1900-08-01\", \"N/A\", \"N/A\", \"N/A\"],\n]\nstored = [(r[0], c, v) for r in rows for c, v in zip(header[1:], r[1:]) if v != \"N/A\"]\nn_dates = len({d for d, _, _ in stored})\nn_rates = len(stored)\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n        path = os.path.join(tmp, \"in.csv\")\n        with open(path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as fh:\n            w = csv.writer(fh, lineterminator=\"\\n\")\n            w.writerow(header + [\"\"])\n            for r in rows:\n                w.writerow(r + [\"\"])\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(\"load\", path)\n    assert code == 0\n    lines = out.splitlines()\n    assert lines[0] == \"dates,rates\"\n    got_dates, got_rates = lines[1].split(\",\")\n    assert int(got_dates) == n_dates\n    assert int(got_rates) == n_rates\n    assert len(lines) == 2\n    for cur in header[1:]:\n        hist = db.rates.list_currency_history(currency=cur)\n        assert [(r.date, r.rate) for r in hist] == sorted((d, v) for d, c, v in stored if c == cur)\n    assert db.rates.list_for_date(date=\"1900-08-01\") == []\n=== END AC load-counts-and-text ===\n\n=== AC load-reload-is-idempotent ===\nIntent: Loading the same file twice reports the same counts both times The header's first field is `Date`; every other field is a three-letter uppercase currency code.\n   The ECB writes a trailing comma on each line; the resulting empty last field is ignored.\n2. Each row has exactly as many fields as the header, and begins with a date `YYYY-MM-DD` that is a\n   real calendar date and is not repeated within the file.\n3. A value is `N/A` (no rate, nothing returned) or matches `[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?` and is nonzero.\n4. The whole file is checked before the function returns; any violation raises `RejectedFile`, a\n   subclass of `Exception`.\n5. Rates are returned as the published text, never as a number.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC parse-valid-file |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261027 V1 |\n| Description | `fxrates load <csv>` validates an ECB history file, stores its rates as text in one transaction, and reports the dates and rates counts. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-LoadValidation.md, FEATURE-InitCommand.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides | fxrates load |\n| Consumes | fxrates_app.ecb.parse_history, rates.replace_rate, fxrates_app.config.database_path, cli.main |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`fxrates load <csv>` reads an ECB history file, validates it completely with\n`fxrates_app.ecb.parse_history`, and then upserts every non-N/A rate through `rates.replace_rate`\nin a single transaction, so a reader sees all or none of the load.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- A rejected file (`RejectedFile`) exits `3`, prints nothing to standard output, writes one\n  diagnostic line to standard error, and stores nothing.\n- On success it prints `dates,rates` on the first line and `<dates>,<rates>` on the second, where\n  `dates` counts the dates in the file with at least one stored rate and `rates` counts the rates stored.\n- A rate for a (date, currency) already stored replaces it, so loading the same file twice leaves the\n  database unchanged.\n- A file holding only N/A values prints `0,0` and stores nothing.\n- Unreadable files are handled by FEATURE-LoadUnreadable.md.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- `FEATURE-RATE-001` — write: store a rate, replacing the stored one - supplies: `date`, `currency`, `rate` - returns: `date`, `currency`, `rate`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC load-counts-and-text ===\nIntent: A valid file exits 0, reports the dates and rates counts derived from the fixture (an all-N/A date is not counted), and stores each rate as published text.\nRequires: newline=\"\") as fh:\n        w = csv.writer(fh, lineterminator=\"\\n\")\n        w.writerow([\"Date\", cur, \"\"])\n        for d, v in rows:\n            w.writerow([d, v, \"\"])\n\nwith nothing: the whole file is validated before the first write.\n- The load is applied in one transaction so a reader never sees a partly applied load.\n- Rates are stored as published text and arithmetic never uses floating point.\n- Standard output carries only the counts; failures write one diagnostic line to standard error.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-LoadUnreadable.md ===\n# FEATURE: Load Unreadable File\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261027 V1 |\n| Description | A missing or unreadable file makes `fxrates load` exit 1 with one stderr line and write nothing. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-LoadStore.md |\n| Provides | fxrates load unreadable-file handling |\n| Consumes | fxrates load |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nOperational failures reading the input file are distinct from a rejected file. An `OSError` (missing\nfile, directory, permission) or undecodable content while reading makes `load` exit `1`, print\nnothing to standard output, write exactly one diagnostic line to standard error, and write nothing\nto the database.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC load-missing-file ===\nIntent: Loading a path that does not exist exits 1 with empty stdout and one stderr line, and adds no rows.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    before = len(db.rates.list_all_keys())\n    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n        path = os.path.join(tmp, \"no-such-file.csv\")\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(\"load\", path)\n    assert code == 1\n    assert out == \"\"\n    assert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n    assert len(db.rates.list_all_keys()) == before\n=== END AC load-missing-file ===\n\n=== AC load-directory-path ===\nIntent: Loading a directory cannot be read as a file, so it exits 1 with empty stdout and one stderr line.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nimport tempfile\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    before = len(db.rates.list_all_keys())\n    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(\"load\", tmp)\n    assert code == 1\n    assert out == \"\"\n    assert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n    assert len(db.rates.list_all_keys()) == before\n=== END AC load-directory-path ===\n\n=== AC load-undecodable-file ===\nIntent: A file whose bytes are not valid text exits 1 with empty stdout and one stderr line, and stores nothing.\nRequires: executable=python3; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    before = len(db.rates.list_all_keys())\n    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n        path = os.path.join(tmp, \"binary.csv\")\n        with open(path, \"wb\") as fh:\n            fh.write(b\"\\xff\\xfe\\xfa\\x80\\x81\\xff\")\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(\"load\", path)\n    assert code == 1\n    assert out == \"\"\n    assert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n    assert len(db.rates.list_all_keys()) == before\n=== END AC load-undecodable-file ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- An unreadable file is exit 1 and never exit 3;
